# Native mobile workflow parity and release evidence

Baseline: **v1.3.2**, commit **ee82a9e3e9a1d4e0c406f5ffff80e768a1f1f812**, frozen 2026-09-22. Execution order and release policy: [migration roadmap](native-mobile-migration.md).

This is the initial source-grounded inventory, not a declaration of native parity. Before closing each row, inspect the baseline behavior and split it if independently supported variants need separate evidence. Omitted baseline capabilities remain in scope. Platform availability follows released behavior, not an assumption that both platforms offer every integration.

## Current iOS status — 2026-10-07

The native iOS app is a development app with substantial local workflow coverage, not a release-ready replacement. The foundation, task browsing/capture, much of the editor, projects, organization, reviews, calendar/board views and selected Settings flows have implementation and focused evidence below. Roughly halfway through visible workflows is a planning estimate, not a measured completion percentage; release readiness is less advanced.

Recent accepted slices include Reference bulk status changes with exact-request recovery and archived-project saved previews, task-to-project promotion, Focus start-date inclusion, Auto-archive, Task Editor layout controls and Reset, RN-shared task opening modes, single Task/Project Trash restore, Project Delete/Undo, Duplicate, Complete/Reactivate, Cancel, single Task/Project Archive Restore, single Task/Project Archive-to-Trash, single Done Task Delete/Undo, quick status and completion-time changes, Archive bulk Restore/Delete/Undo, and Done range selection with bulk Delete/Undo. Remaining work includes other bulk/recovery actions, remaining Settings, file attachments/import/export/backup, sync and encryption, reminders/external calendars, widgets/Siri/Watch/audio/AI, full accessibility/iPad/performance coverage, and signed RN-to-native upgrades with credentials and mixed-client sync. Passing an individual slice does not close its entire workflow row or any release gate. Later slices below add bounded data export/import and backup flows, local Task/Project file attachment operations, Task photo Add and foreground audio playback. The bounded remote attachment checks below do not establish full sync or signed production-upgrade acceptance.

Reference bulk Add and Remove tag are accepted within the bounded core, simulator and physical-device evidence below. The normal/RTL Remove-tag notice assertions remain recorded failures, with separate durable-data and cold-launch evidence. RN does not offer standalone Organize on Reference, so that is not a Reference parity requirement. Task197 adds native Settings → Data diagnostics logging, file sharing and checked clearing, with bounded simulator and iPhone evidence below. Feedback, other support/update flows and the remaining U06 inventory are open.

Task attachment Download is integrated in `ed50b661` using the existing recoverable draft and shared availability policy. Isolated iPhone 12 / iOS 17.5.1 checks covered actual WebDAV bytes, Keep/cold resume, full Save, owned-file Discard cleanup, borrowed-file retention, and delayed-reply background recovery. The delayed XCTest body did not run because its runner stalled during launch; the corresponding physical Appium check passed.

`b8b2d38f` adds selected Task attachment repair after a verified iPhone container UUID change. It reuses hash-verified current files as borrowed draft resources; it never reads or retires the old path. Physical Keep/cold resume/Save and Discard/cold cleanup passed, with original development data preserved across 21 tables and preferences. Twelve focused macOS relocation cases passed across corrected runs, alongside 19 existing passes and one expected platform skip. This does not rebind pending histories. Selected Project relocation repair is recorded below; full provider, mixed-client sync, encryption, and signed RN-upgrade gates remain open.

## How to record progress

Per-platform progression: **N** not implemented/integrated → **I** implemented → **A** automatically checked → **D** device checked → **R** release ready. Stages accumulate evidence. For visual/accessibility checks, record the appropriate human check and why automation is not applicable rather than inventing a test.

**P** means related Android pilot code was reported in ADR 0029; it is not acceptance of the complete workflow. **—** means no native evidence recorded here. **N/A** requires baseline platform unavailability, not an unimplemented native feature. Applicable rows block release until verified or an intentional replacement is explicitly accepted.

RN behavior and regression history are required inputs to each migration slice. Before acceptance, inspect the actual RN screen/controller, its shared policy, relevant tests and fix history; record which prior failure cases have native evidence and which remain open. The frozen baseline defines the inventory, not a reason to omit later RN fixes informed by user feedback. Reusing shared policy alone does not establish interaction or recovery parity, and unfinished native behavior is not an approved difference.

Each evidence link identifies commit, artifact/core hashes, device/OS or test environment, fixture/provider/channel, date, exact check and outcome. Keep sensitive contents/credentials out of reports. Record automated and hands-on results separately; injected taps, simulator results, and existing RN tests are not physical-device native verification. Link failures to an issue, and accepted differences to a dated maintainer decision.

## Workflow inventory

Source pointers locate baseline behavior and reusable checks; they do not claim new clients pass existing tests. Paths are repository-relative and were checked at the frozen commit. Read that revision for the parity definition and the implementation revision for current behavior. Cross-cutting safety and release rows also include new migration acceptance requirements from the roadmap.

iOS foundation evidence (2026-09-27): uncommitted work based on `2d62a09a9ff5cecef08a48554bd43bd614bbd9b6`, macOS 27 / Xcode 27, existing iPhone 18 Pro simulator. Inbox and Focus capture/complete/restart checks passed, as did note/context draft and keyboard-submit checks. Area include/exclude/All cycling and persistence also passed in the simulator. The 49-test Swift/JSC suite passed, including failed-write/restart area selection and two startups on copied RN data; an additional focused Task View paging/read-only test passed. Task details displayed saved Markdown after restart and closed without completing the task. The subsequent text-editor host checks passed failed-save and lost-acknowledgment restart recovery, canonical title matching, same-field conflicts and preservation of dates/checklist/attachments. Simulator editing passed preview, cancel/discard, save and restart. Global Search passed query changes, task edit/completion, completed filters and return-to-Focus checks. The expanded 58-test Swift/JSC suite then passed without skips, with the copied RN fixture enabled. A corrected metadata assertion compares decoded checklist/attachment content while retaining exact date/scalar comparisons; copied RN startup passed again. A further Waiting host test passed people/deferred-project paging and stale reads; simulator Waiting passed person filtering, Search return, edit, completion fallback and restart. Projects and Menu accessibility suppression also passed. The expanded metadata host suite passed all 61 Swift/JSC tests without skips, including invalid Reference metadata rejection and no-write restart replay. Simulator priority/energy editing passed preview, save/restart, discard and empty clearing; the existing text-edit regression also passed with core section expansion. Custom estimates resolved correctly through Preview, another metadata control, and direct Save; restart retained the estimate and unrelated task fields. The Menu adapts its columns at accessibility text sizes. Someday expanded the passing Swift/JSC suite to 62 tests without skips and passed unsigned Debug/Release simulator builds. Its simulator workflow passed grouping, filters, title editing, Search, completion and restart. A post-save keyboard/layout hang passed once after removing a filter-overlay toolbar, then recurred after filter extraction. Explicit editor actions now dismiss focused input before disabling/removing fields. Someday passed three consecutive simulator runs after that correction, and metadata/text editor regressions passed. A 102-task fixture passed all list pages and a token query beyond the first 100 options, including include/exclude/remove, with every task row unchanged. Reference expanded the passing host suite to 65 Swift/JSC tests without skips. Two repeated Reference simulator workflows passed filter/edit/Search/restart, and manual checks passed all task pages, tag search beyond the first 100 options, include/exclude/remove, archived read-only preview, project links/return and Clear. The 103-task fixture and its project remained byte-identical in SQLite during those reads after baseline normalization; original RN files also remained unchanged. An archived/deleted-parent completion guard now refuses new writes while preserving already-done retries. The unsigned Release simulator build passed. History and Trash expanded the passing suite to 68 Swift/JSC tests with the copied RN fixture enabled. Both simulator navigation/restart tests passed. Populated History checks covered three pages per task tab, token search beyond 100 options, cancelled rows and archived task/project previews; Trash covered all 103 static rows, Search return and area exclusion. All 204 History tasks/two projects and 101 Trash tasks/two projects retained their content. The deliberate edit test canonicalized JSON whitespace in two seeded tag arrays; a retained baseline confirmed no semantic changes, and subsequent area browsing left all fixture rows exact. Seven original RN storage files remained unchanged. Unsigned Debug and Release simulator builds passed. Destination editing expanded the passing suite to 73 Swift/JSC tests. Five focused destination tests passed again after adding its validation diagnostic. Simulator destination and metadata regressions passed together, including save/discard/restart. Existing-project, direct-area, section selection/clear, cross-area search and custom-estimate transition checks retained unrelated task fields and all six destination containers. Full association comparison refused direct and recovered saves over another writer’s newly added section. The Release simulator build passed; original RN files and previous History/Trash fixture rows remained unchanged. Creating destinations and automatic stale-section draft clearing remain pending. Contexts/tags now use core normalization, suggestions and quick choices. The 77-test Swift/JSC suite passed, including failed-write/lost-ack token recovery and repeated-prefix retries. Simulator checks passed focused Save, restart, preview/discard, clearing both fields, suggestion focus, the full padded input tap area and largest-text wrapping; an unsigned Release build passed. Date editing now uses prepared core changes and a private durable journal. All 84 Swift/JSC tests passed with copied RN data, as did the date simulator regression and existing destination/metadata/token regressions. Manual native day/time wheel, date-only and clear saves retained exact raw values and unrelated fields through restart; relative scheduling followed core rules. Largest-text controls and the unsigned Release build passed. RN screenshot comparison used the installed v1.3.1 build 4, so it does not close the frozen v1.3.2 visual baseline. No physical iOS evidence or signed replacement evidence is claimed. The app keeps original RN storage untouched; its explicit debug rehearsal renders a staged copy. Remaining data-preservation gates are in [iOS upgrade safety](native-ios-upgrade-safety.md).

## Release decisions

Contexts browsing subsequently passed its final simulator regression and Projects navigation regression. Manual checks covered all 103 task rows, chip search beyond 100 options, area counts, rapid token selection, scroll continuity and largest-text chip layout. A repeated date check exposed focused amount normalization replacing a cleared input; a deterministic regression failed before correction and passed afterward. The separately approved RN timezone fix preserves unchanged timed dates on title-only saves, including recovered JSON drafts. The combined 86-test Swift/JSC suite, 154 focused core tests, 21 desktop tests, core/mobile/desktop typechecks, final date simulator regression and unsigned Release build passed. Original RN files and the earlier preservation fixtures remained unchanged. These results do not close physical or signed-update gates.

Focus controls subsequently passed 89 Swift/JSC tests, 58 focused core tests, core typecheck and simulator filter/edit/Search/restart, initial-read retry, modal containment and rejected-location recovery tests. Manual checks reached all 210 grouped occurrences without moving the current row on More, searched token/project options beyond 100, retained saved filters across area changes and handled rapid selections. Largest-text checks prompted a two-row root header to prevent overlap. Final unsigned Debug/Release builds and all prior data-preservation checks passed. Saved-filter mutation and physical/signed-update gates remain open.

### Isolated physical alpha, 2026-09-27

The iPhone 12 (iOS 17.5.1) ran the separate `tech.dongdongbh.mindwtr.native.dev` Debug build from base `2d62a09a9ff5cecef08a48554bd43bd614bbd9b6` plus the task20 source archive and recorded project-platform override. Binary SHA-256 `c1c6b88e0794831c4b05ff639427a27db6e92904564a6b28ea24b0d588cc29bc`; core bundle SHA-256 `ad71d8bc7438eada4eb0bed41fb797b22c803f3b899ec3e6a4264842eabc4b5c`. Appium/WDA interaction covered capture into Focus, title editing, daily recurrence interval entered while focused, immediate Save, cold restart, native Until wheel save/reopen, calendar preview, completion, Search return and Contexts selection return. A snapshot of its isolated SQLite database showed exactly one completed parent and one next occurrence with the original recurrence series. A second snapshot after cold restart and read navigation had identical values in every task column. Private evidence is retained locally; device logs show prepared recurrence save applied and completion saved.

The production RN 1.3.2 build 4 remained installed under its original identity. This side-by-side alpha uses a separate sandbox; it proves neither ordinary RN replacement-update preservation nor full physical UI parity. The gates in [iOS upgrade safety](native-ios-upgrade-safety.md) remain open.

### iOS Process Inbox write and simulator acceptance (2026-09-27)

The final prepared writer and Swift journal passed all 139 actual Swift/JavaScriptCore tests with the copied RN fixture enabled, no failures or skips. Tests cover failed persistence, lost acknowledgments, exact cold replay, recurring follow-up identity, atomic project conversion, and forged journal refusal before SQLite access. The final iPhone 12/iOS 17.5.1 development build installed over the prior alpha and preserved all 11 tasks, both projects and settings exactly after startup. On that phone, Skip preserved edited title/notes and project conversion created exactly one project and two extra Inbox actions. The expected 13 tasks and three projects survived cold restart with every column unchanged; the nine unrelated prior tasks, both original projects and settings stayed exact. The maintainer-approved shared progress fix retains completed work when conversion or capture grows the queue. Its 72 focused core tests, 96 RN modal tests, fixture capture and core/mobile typechecks passed, as did independent source review. The final 139-test Swift/JSC suite passed; the simulator checked 2/5 after Skip plus conversion and 0/4 on a fresh session. The iPhone showed 1/6 after conversion, and its expected 15 tasks and four projects survived cold restart exactly. All 12 unrelated task rows, three prior projects and settings remained unchanged. Undo, AI assistance, full accessibility coverage and signed production RN replacement remain open.

Checklist update (2026-09-28): the complete editor workflow passed simulator UI103, including Save, Reset preserving unsaved fields, Discard, restart, reorder and History refresh after a Done task returns to Next. Device36 verified the persisted workflow on iPhone 12 / iOS 17.5.1. Preparation and commit use complete saved rows, with adapter, data-epoch and memory-reference checks across asynchronous reads. This preserves legacy terminal Task focus order that normal load removes only from its display state. Full-row and membership comparisons still reject intervening changes. Runtime checks exposed the original memory/raw cold-recovery mismatch; independent review then caught generic persistence retry replacing the saved effect with normalized memory, and an unrelated failed Task write being superseded. All three are corrected. Retry directly flushes the durable effect. An exact operation/failure marker permits settling only that operation's exhausted error; unrelated failures remain blocked before mutation. No schema, loader normalization or comparator weakening was introduced.

Settings General App lock follow-up (2026-09-30, Task102): the shared core owns the canonical device-local security flag, raw presence/value witness, CAS and exact request receipts. Native LocalAuthentication handles device-owner authentication before enabling; disabling does not prompt. No authentication material enters the journal. Security changes create no sync stamp. Startup, backgrounding and uncertain-save recovery conceal private screens and sheets; successful unlock refreshes the selected surface. An exact durable receipt resolves a cold retry after intervening edits. Without it, explicit cancellation abandons the unknown journal and resumes the canonical saved flag without reapplying an old authenticated request.

Task Editor Assigned To (2026-09-30, Task112): native reuses the existing suggestion input lifecycle and shared person suggestions for the field offered by core layout. Assignment text participates in v2 prepared saves and combined checklist saves; old plain/v1 journals remain sealed. Shared serialization trims/clears the value. Queries longer than 2,000 UTF-16 units skip suggestions and retain their complete text for shared edit/save validation. No new writer, command, schema or dependency. Status controls, Waiting For transition and inline New Person remain separate work.

Core focused coverage passed 249/249 and canonical checks passed 9 with one expected skip; core/mobile/desktop typechecks, lint and Android boot passed. Independent shared-core and native source reviews found no remaining production defect before runtime validation. Linux/Mac official bundle SHA-256 matched `0293ea3f992e00b5afe6186f6fea74244139cfde10f228df15ba817f301a3fde`. Native validation passed 23 existing recovery/compatibility cases, then the corrected new suggestion/Unicode case in 0.423s. Its initial assertions incorrectly expected exact-name suggestions and compared unordered JSON object encoding; both test-only assumptions were corrected. Simulator screenshot inspection then exposed omitted shared translation keys for the label and placeholder; those keys were added before final device validation. No full-suite claim.

Assignment saves do not explicitly create a Person. The existing shared load normalization can derive a Person from an unfamiliar saved assignment on restart, as RN does. Cold-restart preservation checks therefore distinguish this expected derivation from changes to existing People or unrelated rows.

## Approved Android native differences (maintainer decision, 2026-10-01)

The maintainer approved these native Android behaviors that differ from the React Native app ("approve the native-only differences listed in the handoff, including C1's three", 2026-10-01):

- App lock also hides the app's screenshot in the recent-apps view (stricter than RN).
- Buttons dim while a command runs or while a failed save waits for its retry.
- The capture screen locks its fields while a save is owed. Cancel keeps an owed retry, which may still land after the journal replay.
- The capture screen's Back for a warm `capture-quick` start matches RN's.
- Settings › AI: non-secret text fields send their change 500 ms after typing pauses (RN writes every keystroke). The key and base URL fields write each change at once, in order.
- Process Inbox's AI toast "Open" closes Process Inbox, then opens Settings › AI (RN pushes the route under its modal; that RN behavior is a suspected bug, not yet verified).
- A Settings row's text wraps about 12 dp wider than RN's.
- Attachments (approved 2026-10-01): a picked image keeps its original bytes; RN's picker re-encodes it at quality 0.9.
- Project details (approved 2026-10-04): the area and tag pickers, the section manager and the help are full-screen overlays with RN's scrim, drawn over the whole screen; the tab bar stays visible on the open project's screen (RN's project screen is a full-screen modal).


Task Editor backdated completion (2026-10-01, Task115): the native editor offers an accessible secondary completion-time action with a native date/time wheel. Shared TypeScript helpers own opening fallback, instant validation and optional minutes normalization, and RN consumes the same helpers. Open and Cancel preserve parent draft inputs; Confirm resolves parent inputs and applies one session-fenced draft edit. Save uses the existing prepared checklist transaction. Unchanged picker values retain seconds and milliseconds. No new writer, schema or dependency.

Core/mobile/desktop typechecks, core lint (three pre-existing warnings) and the Bun Android boot gate passed. Independent production review found no remaining defect. Four related status/minutes/checklist regressions passed in 27.747s. Official Task115 bundle SHA-256 `9e6548d81663c9da66f755e8b987c8535fb293d7ae85a7db0a181e3c01536bb6`.

The final dialog keeps one scroll form mounted across keyboard resizing, uses available height at accessibility text sizes, and retains 48-point action targets. Earlier navigation, keyboard-focus and storage failures remain in private evidence.

Durability begins after the latest checkpoint completes and the protected indicator appears; a kill before acknowledgment may lose the last input. Queued-operation recovery and late-callback fences have shared/host coverage; the compound UI test does not force an unresolved schedule queue across process death. Physical authentication remains unavailable by maintainer choice. Full editor parity and release gates remain open.

Task Editor inline New Person (2026-10-01, Task117): Assigned To now offers RN's inline creation action. Shared core decides eligibility from the displayed suggestions and returns the current Person display name. The existing prepared Person writer owns creation and exact retry; it never saves the parent Task. A checkpoint precedes submission, and the acknowledged Person remains after Task Discard. A failed post-write read or identity mismatch blocks Task Save until a successful read or explicit assignment edit; retrying that read never repeats creation.

Task Editor automatic text direction (2026-10-01, Task118): title and Notes use the same shared `resolveAutoTextDirection` policy as RN, combining both raw inputs with the current language. The bounded read-only host route rejects malformed input and unavailable Tasks without changing storage. Native results are accepted only for the same Task, recovery session, raw input pair and language. Presentation failures retain the last direction and do not alter Save, Retry or draft checkpoints. Only the two inputs change writing direction; the form stays in its existing layout.

The iOS 26+ implementation explicitly uses layout-based writing direction and alignment because the newer SDK otherwise lets each input's own paragraph direction override the combined policy. iOS 16–25 retains its compatible layout-direction path. Eleven focused core direction/resume checks, core typecheck, focused lint and a Swift/JSC read-only check passed. The host check compared all nine tables and observed no SQL or journal writes. UI813 passed normal-size Save/Discard and cold reopen in 169.301s: Save wrote one Task, Discard wrote none, unrelated tables stayed exact, and no journal or draft remained. Screenshots confirmed Latin title/Arabic Notes and Arabic title/English Notes align right, then English-only inputs align left. UI809–811 retained test-helper failures; UI812 passed interaction/data checks but exposed the iOS 26 visual mismatch, corrected before UI813.

Task Editor URL attachments (2026-10-01, Task119): Add link accepts the shared multiline `Title | URL` format; Edit and Remove update only the Task draft. One prepared Task Save merges the original attachment baseline with the latest saved list and commits links together with scalar/checklist changes. The complete merged effect remains frozen for exact retry. Live file records cannot be changed through the URL-only path, including with a forged baseline. Original bases, confirmed draft links and unfinished sheet input survive cold recovery; Discard invokes no attachment-file cleanup. The existing 1 MB draft checkpoint cap remains, with a visible retryable error and Save blocked on checkpoint failure.

Shared-core implementation checks passed 167 tests with two skipped; after the independent-review title correction, 139 affected tests passed with two skipped. Desktop paths show the shared mobile explanation; unsupported schemes show the shared generic failure. The URL-only route does not bind a file host, write the Task or create a command journal.

Saved-search Save/Delete (2026-10-01, Task128): Global Search saves a trimmed name/query or opens the existing exact-query match unchanged. Saved Search offers Delete with cancellation. Nested Saved Search → Search → another Saved Search returns through each original caller. Shared prepared commands freeze the full raw collection and a device-local monotonic `savedSearchesUpdatedAt` stamp. Exact replay is a no-op; later rename, deletion, recreation or equal-list edits refuse stale commands. Unrelated settings and unknown saved-search properties remain intact. The stamp does not join synced settings or add a SQLite column.

Task Share (2026-10-01, Task131): the Task editor’s More menu opens the native activity sheet using a shared formatter extracted from RN’s existing handler. The message retains RN’s order, labels, feature flags, dates, description and checklist formatting, including unsaved editor fields. A bounded read validates the Task revision and read-only state. The native caller resolves current inputs and waits for the existing draft checkpoint before the OS handoff, rejects changed sessions/drafts or a concealed app, and clears the sheet on app lock. Cancel retains edits; Share creates no Task write or command journal. Core task-view/share checks passed 38/38 and RN hook checks passed 17/17; core/mobile typechecks, targeted mobile lint and core lint passed (three existing core warnings). The real JavaScriptCore/SQLite Share check passed in 0.542s, covering unsaved text, exact no-write comparison, stale/malformed refusal and a fresh host. Independent review and the title-metadata follow-up found no blocker.

Refusal retains the draft; uncertain failure retains both the frozen draft and command. Cold recovery opens the verified copy through the existing Board recovery route. No new core production writer was added.

Skip recurring occurrence (2026-10-01, Task138): Task editor More offers the existing RN action for eligible saved fixed-schedule tasks. It resolves the current draft, archives/cancels that occurrence, and creates at most one canonical next occurrence in one prepared checklist Save effect. It does not complete the source or offer a new Undo action. Ineligible drafts receive the existing Save-or-discard guidance. RN and native reuse the same Skip planner; native freezes IDs, dates and affected rows for exact retries and cold recovery. Validation also checks the next anchor and independent sibling calendar days through the shared recurrence projector in the preparation timezone. Frozen sub-day and derived relative-start projections retain the existing prepared-completion trust boundary; this does not authenticate arbitrary rewritten journals.

Task opening mode (2026-10-02, Task147): Task Editor now offers the shared Automatic/Preview/Edit choices, stored only in the existing device-local preference scope. The RN resolver was moved unchanged into shared core and re-exported from RN; native invokes that same precedence before publishing the editor. Ordinary Inbox taps default to Edit, other ordinary opens and internal task references default to Preview, explicit Edit actions retain Edit, and read-only archived-project tasks stay in Preview. An ordinary archived task is not automatically read-only: RN's Archived-list caller supplies no read-only flag. Manual tab selection and a recovered draft's frozen tab take precedence over a later preference change. Reset to Default preserves this preference. The explicit staged RN rehearsal imports its legacy value only when the native scoped key is absent; invalid or oversized stored values fall back through shared Automatic policy. No new SQLite setting, sync stamp, write journal or dependency.

RN provenance: actual `task-open-mode.ts`, `TaskEditModal`, `TaskList`, Inbox, markdown task links and `openTaskScreen`; introduction `5ee6f6535` (#1227) and shared choices/key/read rule `2d62a09a9`. This audit caught the old native Inbox Preview default and prevented accidentally applying the Inbox default to a linked task. Focused core checks passed 153 tests; RN resolver/hydration checks passed 18 tests with a worktree-local alias (the ordinary symlinked dependency resolves main). Core/mobile typechecks, diagnostic-field checks and core lint passed (three existing task-status warnings). Swift covered 26 Task Editor/opening-mode cases: 25 passed initially, and the one older exact-shape assertion was corrected for the added openMode projection and passed on rerun. Strict read boundaries reject malformed input/output and write no database or journal.

Single Project restore from Trash (2026-10-02, Task150): leading swipe and explicit Restore tap now apply RN's shared Project restore effect. Project identity/content/lifecycle are retained; only children with the Project's original cascade deletion timestamp are restored. Earlier-deleted and purged children stay deleted. Current Project Delete detaches live Tasks, so Trash Restore does not reattach them; Undo is a separate operation with a saved relationship snapshot. Existing live/restored Sections determine restored Task section references, and shared Area repair preserves or clears the Project's Area/title. The prepared writer binds full Project/owned Task/Section membership, Area and device witnesses and performs one guarded save. No new schema, dependency or alternate Swift restore policy.

Coverage includes atomic failed COMMIT, complete child membership guards, nonterminal lost-acknowledgment replay with unchanged versus edited children, durable terminal acknowledgment after later edits, and forged journals rejected before SQLite.

Project Delete and Undo (2026-10-02, Task151): the Project detail action menu now exposes RN’s explicit Delete confirmation, including archived Projects. A durable Delete tombstones the Project/live Sections and detaches live Tasks without removing their contents or lifecycle. The 5.2-second session-local Undo uses current Task contents, restores eligible container links, and skips Tasks refiled or deleted since Delete. Confirmation binds the displayed Project ID/revision; active Notes/rename edits and sheets block the action. Failed saves retain the exact request; cold recovery routes to Projects without inventing an RN Undo toast after restart.

RN provenance: `ProjectDetailModal`/`projects-screen`, #1142 menu change `068684472`, relationship-Undo fix `15606d72b`, shared `deleteProject`, `projectRestoreEffect`, `undo-project-delete`, and `batchUpdateTasks` container/order/update helpers. Review found and corrected incomplete section-only membership witnesses and omitted RN order reservation. A direct differential test verifies saved orders 8/2 plus an intervening Project task at 12 become 13/14 in both RN and native while later content survives. Source review passed after those corrections. Eight new core tests, four targeted existing RN tests (225 filtered skips), 152 adjacent native/diagnostic tests, core typecheck and lint passed (three existing task-status warnings). Canonical-read plus diagnostic checks passed 11 tests with one existing skip. The Task150 missing canonical-write registration was separately fixed and pushed as `195f85747`; its CI run `36978491418` passed.

Seven final-bundle Swift/JSC/SQLite tests passed (25.788 seconds), covering failed COMMIT, exact retry, changed-child refusal, terminal acknowledgment after edits and malformed journals rejected before SQLite. Initial physical compilation exceeded Swift’s type-check budget in a long optional-result chain; splitting the expression preserved precedence and the corrected build signed successfully. A first UI failure came from duplicate iOS 27 alert accessibility wrappers before the action; the test now selects the first matching wrapper. Visual review then caught a missing preloaded confirmation translation key, fixed with an exact body-text assertion. Runtime acceptance is recorded below.

The UI uses Complete rather than Archive (#1165), stored status remains archived, and no confirmation is added, matching RN’s reversible action and iOS page-sheet behavior. Project Cancel remains separate pending work.

Shared host validator stack correction (2026-10-02, Task156): private native CI `36991357204` and an isolated rerun of `testProjectAddAreaFreshCreateThenAssignPreservesRichRows` both crashed with SIGBUS and “Thread stack size exceeded” on the 544 KiB native-core worker. Compiled Debug prologs reserve 163,248 bytes in `arguments`, 90,768 in `journalArguments`, 222,480 in `call(editorAttempt)` and 36,128 in `finish`: 512,624 bytes across those four nested frames before the remaining validation and Foundation calls. ISO8601 parsing was the final stack consumer, not an invalid-date assertion. The correction targets shared validation dispatch rather than increasing stack size or changing date semantics. The accepted runtime evidence follows below.

The shared dispatchers now retain all original guards in ordered method-family helpers. Independent source review found no lost guard, changed error order, journal fallback or legacy `allowPreparedDates:false` bypass. Compiled Debug reservations on the Area acknowledgment path now total 321,024 bytes (about 313.5 KiB): `arguments` 1,104, `journalArguments` 5,424, Area journal helper 21,744, Project field helper 34,096, `call` 222,480 and `finish` 36,176. This recovers about 187 KiB without increasing stack size or changing date parsing. The unchanged formerly crashing Area test passed (0.676 seconds), and all 35 focused Area/lifecycle/status/legacy-date tests passed (120.749 seconds) from a fresh Mac checkout. The rebuilt core bundle matches Task155’s SHA-256 exactly. Full validation and physical evidence are recorded below.

Review Task row actions (2026-10-02, Task159): native Due and All overview rows now expose RN's Mark reviewed and Review in 1 week only when the shared core marks the Task due. The selected action retains the displayed Task revision. Mark reviewed clears only reviewAt; advancing preserves date-only values and the original local clock on timed values. Task status, recurrence and other contents remain unchanged. Core validation: four focused suites passed 104 tests, including whole-row equality against RN updates, pure preparation, a writer between reads, frozen-midnight commit, real SQLite failed COMMIT, recreated-host replay and intervening-edit refusal. Archived Task Restore (2026-10-02, Task172): History > Archived > Tasks now exposes an explicit leading-swipe Restore action; swiping alone does not restore. It reuses RN `moveArchivedTaskToInbox` / `updateTask` preparation and parent effects: completed and cancelled Tasks return to Inbox with the same identity; an archived parent Project reactivates, owned Sections recover, and sibling archive markers retire without changing sibling status. Task content and recurrence settings remain intact; Restore does not complete a Task or generate a recurrence child. Legacy Section-only ownership and obsolete Area assignments follow the same RN container resolution.

A dedicated prepared envelope freezes the displayed revision, affected parent membership and date normalization. One atomic save commits the effect and exact UUID receipt; failed writes retain the same journal for retry. Cold validation runs before SQLite activation, and saved receipts acknowledge lost replies without overwriting later edits. Unproven saves keep the write gate and localized recovery message. This adds single-Task Archive Restore, not bulk restore or full History/W07 parity.

Archived Project Restore (2026-10-02, Task173): History > Archived > Projects reuses the existing prepared Project lifecycle Reactivate operation through an explicit leading-swipe Restore button. RN authority is `archived.tsx` `handleRestoreProject` → `reactivateArchivedProject` → `updateProject(id, { status: "active" })`. The row supplies its displayed Project revision; the prepared effect includes child membership and preserves the same RN Task/Section archive-provenance rules. Interactive acknowledgment refreshes History; cold lifecycle recovery opens the restored Project detail through the existing route.

The existing lifecycle receipt is now durable on iOS, and an unused request UUID must match the before-state rather than merely an already-equal result. History Retry retains its original request and requires an exact saved receipt even when journal retry returns an acknowledgment. Missing proof keeps the write gate. The prepared v1 envelope is unchanged.

Upgrade limitation: before Task173, iOS Project lifecycle receipts were process-local. An older pending v1 journal whose data committed without a terminal acknowledgment can therefore have no durable receipt. The new host retains that journal and refuses to infer success from equal rows. This safe refusal is a known blocker for unrestricted production upgrade acceptance, not a claim that legacy pending operations automatically recover. No production release or upgrade acceptance is established by this development slice.

Archive Task to Trash (2026-10-02, Task174): the RN source is `archived.tsx` `handleDelete` → shared `getArchiveConfirmation` → `deleteTask`, including the error reporting added by `e7735a51e`. Native reuses the existing Task Delete v1 pipeline with an explicit private `source: "archive"`; absent source retains editor behavior. Archive Delete requires the displayed archived row revision and a strict before-state CAS, permits that row's archived parent without weakening the editor guard, and does not reactivate the parent or change siblings. The existing core trash planner preserves content, recurrence and container assignments while stamping the soft-delete fields. A fresh UUID cannot claim matching after-state; retries require the exact saved UUID/payload receipt.

Initial Task174 validation passed 15 focused core cases (seven Archive and eight existing editor Delete/Undo), typecheck, lint with zero errors and three existing warnings, generated-bundle boot gates and 22-locale checks. The core differential tests run actual RN `deleteTask` for parented, cancelled and independent archived rows. Mac host, UI/device and independent-review results are recorded below.

Prior Task172 private CI run `37054004795` completed both Apple jobs successfully. Its full native host run executed 589 tests with one skip and zero failures, followed by a successful SwiftUI simulator build. This is Task172 baseline evidence, not Task174 acceptance.

Task174 native host checks passed five new recovery/refusal tests (19.825 seconds) and three existing editor Delete/Undo tests (12.490 seconds). Independent frozen-source review found no blocker. UI1154 stopped before a write because SwiftUI exposed nested Delete alert buttons to the test's label query; all nine tables remained exact. The helper now selects the confirmation identifier's first match, and UI1159 passed three deletes, Trash navigation and cold reopening. The fresh-process cold data comparison below establishes the matching saved result.

The public replacement CI run `37058809726` passed all jobs on `dcc0ae724`, including Core Package, Mobile App and E2E. This validates the Task172 CI repairs and Task173 baseline, not this uncommitted Task174 slice.

Task174 cold validation caught an oracle error: RN's shared `clearDeletedTaskProjectArchiveMetadataMigration` clears three archive-provenance fields from a deleted Task without changing its revision. Reusing `resetForTests()` in the same process leaves cached entities alive, allowing reconciliation to reuse the old unchanged-revision row. The corrected oracle checkpoints and closes the expected database, starts a fresh Bun process for ordinary RN `fetchData` and flush, then compares every row in all nine domain tables. Both UI1159 and the physical cold snapshot matched exactly with three receipts; no fields were waived and no production change was required. Earlier failed oracle outputs are retained.

UI1160, using a fresh UUID library and bounded scrolling to the inline Retry, passed two failed-save retries with all nine tables unchanged and the original journal retained. After removing only the owned fault trigger, UI1161 passed cold recovery and another reopen; the fresh-process RN oracle matched all nine tables with one receipt.

Archive Project to Trash (2026-10-02, Task175): native History follows RN `archived.tsx` `handleDeleteProject` → shared confirmation → `deleteProject`. The existing Project Delete v1 request adds private `source: "archive"`, requiring the displayed archived Project revision and strict before-state CAS. It soft-deletes the Project and live owned Sections, detaches live direct/Section-linked Tasks without recreating them or changing their status/content, and preserves independent tombstones. Confirmation freezes the displayed ID/revision and core-supplied copy; Cancel writes nothing. Interactive success stays in History; cold recovery uses the existing Projects route. No new Undo affordance, schema, sync policy or attachment cleanup was added.

The existing `preparedProjectDelete` command now retains durable exact UUID/payload receipts. Archive retries never infer success from matching rows; unused UUIDs require the complete before-state and membership. Existing detail Delete requests retain their parser and equal-after fallback, while new durable receipts also allow their exact lost-reply recovery to acknowledge safely after later edits. Legacy pending journals without a saved receipt retain their prior limitations; this is not production-upgrade acceptance.

Existing detail regression results follow below.

Done Task Delete and Undo (2026-10-02, Task176): RN `done.tsx` uses the ordinary TaskList, whose Delete checks the shared read-only rule; `swipeable-task-item.tsx` deletes immediately and offers a 5.2-second Undo. Native History Done now uses a List with an explicit trailing Delete action and the existing Undo notice. Swiping alone writes nothing, archived-parent rows stay read-only, and status controls remain unchanged. The existing Task Delete v1 envelope adds private `source: "done"`, binds the displayed revision/status, and requires the exact before-state for Delete and Undo. Shared `deleteTask`/`restoreTask` policy retains task identity, content, containers and recurrence without generating another occurrence. Exact UUID/payload receipts prove retries; equal after-state under an unused UUID does not.

The existing Undo command now retains durable receipts and exposes a pure receipt probe. Native retains the confirmed envelope for retry without inventing a new operation. Failed saves keep the original journal and gate other writes; interactive success stays in History. Undo cold recovery uses the existing startup contract and does not add a new startup acknowledgment shape. The diagnostic `v1.3.4/ios-done-task-trash` records confirmed Delete without task content.

Actual failed-COMMIT simulator recovery exposed a legacy-row bug: normal load adds `pushCount: 0` in memory without saving, while raw recovery reads the unchanged SQLite NULL. The strict prepared-row comparison rejected its own journal. The existing shared Board comparator now treats only missing pushCount and its established zero default as equivalent; other fields, revision checks and UUID attribution remain strict. The SQLite test harness now explicitly supports raw recovery activation, and a genuine close/reopen regression failed before this fix and passed afterward. Concurrent positive/negative counts, title, revision and recurrence changes still refuse.

Core typecheck, lint, 12 API parity tests, generated-bundle boot gates and 22-locale checks passed. Six Swift host tests passed in 47.684 seconds; three existing editor Delete/Undo tests passed in 12.737 seconds. Host checks cover failed Delete/Undo COMMIT, exact retry, cold first apply, terminal/lost reply after later edits, changed-row refusal, unused UUIDs and forged journals before SQLite.

Done Task quick status (2026-10-02, Task177): RN `SwipeableTaskItemStatusMenu.tsx` offers Inbox, Next, Waiting, Someday, Done and Reference; `task-list.tsx` sends the choice to shared `updateTask(id, {status})`. Native History Done now offers the same choices and selected Done state. The menu retains the displayed revision; choosing Done or dismissing writes nothing. Done-to-other does not offer completion Undo, matching RN. Existing Delete/Undo remains available. Read-only, changed, deleted and projected rows cannot write.

Preparation reuses the shared update and lifecycle planners, binding the complete raw source separately from its normal-load projection in a v2 envelope. Pure validation verifies both sources and the frozen single-task effect. Commit rechecks the raw before-state and current container/settings guards. The existing raw Task save overlay preserves untouched legacy ordering and counter fields; a dedicated instance of the existing receipt helper and Area save guard owns failed-save retries. Exact UUID/payload receipts acknowledge landed writes after later edits; an unused UUID cannot claim an equal after-state. No new schema or persistence framework was introduced. Diagnostics use `v1.3.4/ios-done-task-status` only after durable confirmation.

Runtime testing found and corrected two defects before acceptance: the experimental v1 preparation dropped legacy Done focus ordering and failed cold recovery; generic same-session retry could replace raw persistence with normalized memory. Fresh v2 preparation from a copy of the same original failed database now survives failed COMMIT and a new-process replay. Raw focus order changed without a revision bump correctly refuses recovery. Original uncommitted v1 journals, which never bound the missing raw value, remain gated; exact saved v1 receipts still acknowledge. Original UI1180 failure evidence is retained.

Done completion-time editing (2026-10-02, Task178): the RN source is `swipeable-task-item.tsx` edit-mode `applyCompletedAt` → `updateTask(id, {completedAt})`, with `completed-at-picker.tsx` and the shared backdate resolver defining the date policy. Native Done metadata now opens the platform date/time wheel with localized Save/Cancel. The displayed task revision remains frozen; dismissing writes nothing. An unchanged timestamp still writes, matching actual RN revision/updatedAt behavior, while its seconds and milliseconds remain exact. No time-spent edit, completion Undo or recurring child is introduced. Shared automatic archiving follows the earlier #959 correction when the edited date crosses the configured retention boundary.

The existing guarded Done-row writer now serves exactly two actions. Completion edits have their own V2 kind and receipt namespace; public status V1/V2 envelopes remain unchanged. The raw source, its normal-load projection, frozen completedAt-only effect and current settings/container guards must agree. Exact UUID/payload receipts prove landed retries, including after later edits; an unused UUID cannot claim matching after-state. Failed-save recovery retains the original raw effect and journal. `v1.3.4/ios-done-completion-time` logs only a durably confirmed outcome.

Runtime and review caught three production issues before acceptance: missing bootstrap exports made the metadata/recovery accessibility copy empty; Foundation's ordinary Gregorian formatter shifted pre-cutover dates and lost extended/BCE year notation; and the generic editor validator rejected changed extended-year instants despite the row action accepting canonical ISO. Labels now use the existing core translations. The shared instant codec uses Foundation's proleptic Gregorian formatter and strict JS-compatible year formatting; local calendar-wheel formatting is unchanged. Only the private completion intent uses the shared canonical instant resolver, leaving general editor validation unchanged. The tracked `check-task-date-codec.mjs`, included in local Apple validation, executes the actual Swift helper against JavaScript epochs, round trips, forward/backward minute edits and malformed inputs.

Archived Task completion-time editing (2026-10-02, Task179): native Archive metadata now opens the existing platform date/time picker. RN `archived.tsx` → `setArchivedTaskCompletedAt` → `updateTask(id, {completedAt})` remains the policy authority. Actual RN comparisons established three important container behaviors. Archive metadata permits an archived parent even though its ordinary task editor is read-only. A direct edit intentionally clears the four reversible project-archive markers, preventing a later project restore from undoing that edit. A retained archived section fails shared container validation; native preserves that refusal. Ordinary RN load repairs a deleted-parent reference before editing, so the detached task can save; raw recovery with the dangling parent still refuses. No shared store or container policy was changed to force a successful test.

The 19 focused Archive tests, all 32 unchanged Done status/completion tests and 276 adjacent checklist/prepared-save/recurrence/editor tests passed (327 bounded cases). Core typecheck and configured lint passed with zero errors and three existing warnings. Both native bundles, boot gates and two diagnostic-field checks passed. Independent source review found no production blocker. These are bounded checks, not a full core-suite result.

Tests cover failed COMMIT twice, exact retry, frozen cold replay, legacy raw fields, later edits/lost acknowledgment, stale raw state, unused UUID equal-after refusal and archived-parent policy.

Archive task selection and bulk Restore (2026-10-02, Task180): native Archive Tasks now has Select/Done, individual selection, Select all and Restore to Inbox. RN `archived.tsx` and `use-task-list-selection.ts` remain the behavior reference. Select all materializes every currently visible filtered and unfolded task ID, including rows beyond the loaded page. Later reads prune that fixed set; newly arriving tasks are not silently selected. Selection blocks ordinary row editing, swipes and completion-time edits. Projects remain outside task selection. Successful Restore exits selection without a confirmation or Undo, matching RN. Bulk Delete and its Undo were accepted separately in Task181 below.

The real RN `batchUpdateTasks` now uses extracted shared preflight and effect planners. They preserve validation precedence, storage-array order, project ordering, recurrence deduplication and one coordinated parent reactivation. Native uses those same planners for one prepared batch, binding selected revisions and complete raw parent membership, including siblings and sections. Failed saves retain the exact request and effect. A durable UUID/payload receipt proves acknowledgment before mutable-state checks; matching after-state with an unused UUID does not. A batch that exceeds the existing 2 MB journal limit is refused before any write with guidance to select fewer tasks. The 10,000-ID selection bound does not promise that 10,000 rich tasks fit one journal.

Bounded core validation passed 24 new planner/family tests, 96 list-view tests, 229 unchanged store tests, 31 project-reactivation tests, 62 adjacent History tests and two diagnostic checks. Typecheck, configured lint (zero errors, three existing warnings), both native bundles and boot gates passed. Independent review caught and verified correction of an unrelated Trash paging argument before the Mac build; no other source blocker remained. These are bounded checks, not a full core-suite pass.

Canonical all-nine-table comparisons match actual RN. A separate legacy representation test deliberately reports a strict raw difference: RN's full snapshot save changes an unrelated NULL `pushCount` to zero, while the native raw overlay preserves NULL. Every other cell matches, and fresh normal-load AppData is equal. The existing attachment codec's absent `updatedAt` to empty-string conversion is explicitly asserted, rather than described as byte-for-byte preservation. No global codec, comparator or persistence policy was changed.

Archive bulk Delete and Undo (2026-10-02, Task181): Archive task selection now offers Delete with a frozen selection/count confirmation and the existing timed Undo notice. Cancel writes nothing. Successful Delete leaves selection mode; Undo restores the same task records using current shared container sanitization, without recurrence or parent reactivation. RN `archived.tsx`, `batchDeleteTasks`, `restoreTask`, their tests and fix history are the reference. The existing RN task mutation formula is now a shared pure planner; the native prepared family uses that planner over the existing History load projection while retaining exact raw BEFORE authority. Selected legacy focusOrder/pushCount representation changes match RN; unrelated raw rows remain untouched. This is not a promise that RN whole-snapshot normalization preserves every legacy raw cell.

Delete and Undo use selected-row atomic writes, exact durable receipts, retained requests and cold replay. Undo requires the successful Delete receipt and current raw rows, and binds the current container scope. Deleted/missing containers are sanitized without resurrection; archived parents remain valid. Prospective Undo must fit the existing journal bounds before Delete can commit. Oversized operations refuse before writes; no partial batches or target-equality acknowledgments are introduced. Outcome-only release diagnostics use `v1.3.4/ios-archive-bulk-trash`.

Eleven real Swift/JSC host tests passed in 51.608 seconds. The first invocation skipped all eleven because the bundle environment variable was missing; it is not a pass. ## Task198 JSON backup export

Settings → Data → Backup now exports JSON through RN's `getInMemoryAppDataSnapshot`, `serializeBackupData` and filename policy. This includes hidden/history records according to the shared snapshot and preserves attachment metadata; attachment bytes are not bundled. Pending or failed saves, sandbox mode and workspace transitions refuse export without flushing or acknowledging an owed write.

The iOS host writes and closes a unique private file before presenting the system share sheet. Cancellation and stale Data sessions discard their own files. Startup removes interrupted exports only after acquiring the existing exclusive host lock, only from the strict owned filename/directory family, without following links or removing unknown content. The release diagnostic proves file preparation, not delivery to another app.

## Task200 TaskNotes ZIP export

Data → Backup → Export as TaskNotes now uses RN's `buildTaskNotesExportZip` with the full shared snapshot. The shared format excludes Reference, deleted and purged tasks, maps statuses and frontmatter, and produces one Markdown entry per supported live task. This is an explicit one-time export, not vault synchronization or a complete backup.

The read payload declares UTF-8 or base64 encoding. The native host validates the encoding and filename for the requested format, decodes ZIP bytes strictly, then uses the same private, immutable export-file owner as JSON/CSV. Binary bytes never pass through UTF-8 conversion. Pending writes, workspace transitions, stale sessions, cancellation and interrupted-export cleanup retain their previous restrictions. Diagnostics record only the prepared format.

## Task201 JSON import persistence foundations

This prepares the remaining D02 merge/restore work; it does not expose an import action yet. RN remains the policy owner through `applyImportSource`, also used by the existing RN import transaction. Native can commit a frozen document and its exact request receipt together without relying on store snapshot generations. The adapter requires a current durable baseline and the existing concurrent-write fence; an existing matching receipt returns its original result without replacing intervening edits.

The native file owner copies a selected JSON file once with security-scoped access and file coordination. It enforces RN's 128 MiB limit against actual opened bytes, promotes a private durable copy, and verifies its SHA-256 on reopen. Startup cleanup requires validated pending-command ownership and retains all referenced copies; uncertain journal ownership must skip cleanup.

Independent Sol review found three blockers: vanished receipt proof could permit another application, failure diagnostics could include backup-controlled IDs, and the commit marker was not forced with logging off. One correction pass addressed all three, and the coordinator inspected the corrections. The real-SQLite receipt suites passed 78 checks, including COMMIT reaching SQLite before its acknowledgment throws and replay preserving later edits. Existing SQLite checks passed 80 tests, shared import/transaction/backup checks passed 49, and diagnostic-field checks passed two. Core typecheck and scoped lint passed.

Six Swift file-owner tests passed on the Mac with matching source hashes, including the exact 128 MiB boundary, growing input, Unicode, tampered bytes, symlinks, failed promotion, restart and retained-reference cleanup. The final rebuilt JavaScriptCore bundle passed 13 existing backup/diagnostics host regressions alongside the six file-owner checks (19 total). The simulator application built successfully; all 44 Swift source/bundle hashes matched the Mac inputs and the built application contained the final bundle. Boot gates, date-codec checks and 218 governance checks also passed. These host regressions exercise existing paths through the changed adapter; the new document receipt API is covered by real-SQLite core tests, not yet by an iOS merge command. Picker presentation, preview/cancel, recovery snapshot retention, journal integration, merge results and exact-snapshot Undo remain unimplemented in the native UI. Package-level file tests do not establish simulator or physical-device import acceptance.


## Task202 JSON backup merge and recovery

Settings → Data now exposes JSON Merge Backup, shared preview/counts, recovery snapshot selection, and destructive snapshot Undo. It reuses RN parsing, merge and snapshot-restoration policy rather than translating that policy into Swift. Confirm prepares against the latest durable state; selected provider bytes are copied once, and a compact journal refers to a verified immutable plan and snapshot. The document and request receipt commit together. Read-only receipt proof resolves lost acknowledgments without applying the document again. Restore warns that subsequent edits also roll back. Recovery snapshots retain the latest five plus active operation references.

Independent Sol storage/host/core/UI reviews and their correction pass covered stale preparation, missing terminal proof, activation ordering, cancelled pickers, stale screen sessions and cleanup ownership. Boot gates and 218 governance checks passed.

Only simulator builds accept a missing readback; physical iOS still requires the exact complete-until-first-authentication policy.

The maintenance was preserved rather than overwriting current DEV data with an old capture.

These checks establish the bounded JSON merge/recovery flow, not CSV import, other import formats, attachment-byte backup, every file provider, or full D02 parity. ## Task212 native attachment file IO foundation

## Task220 shared Apple exclusive-publication facade

The public Swift package facade now exposes RN's existing immutable-stage preparation, snapshot, exclusive publication and owned-stage cleanup methods. The engine algorithms and Expo bindings are unchanged. Unlike the ordinary installer's compatibility behavior, exclusive publication returns `alreadyExists` for a preexisting target, including identical bytes, without adopting it or consuming the owned stage. Returned identity tokens are filesystem evidence; the caller still has to record them durably before relying on them for draft recovery.

All 39 Mac package tests passed, including eleven public-consumer cases covering recreation, collisions, stale/replaced stages and directories, invalid inputs, symlinks and cleanup. An initial test-only closure-capture compilation error was corrected before the passing run. A signed iPhone development build including this facade passed; no device installation or interaction is claimed. Native draft-session ownership, editor Save/Discard settlement and picker/viewer support remain separate work.


## Task221 native owned-stage streaming

Native file IO now provides a same-descriptor cache-source snapshot and a typed operation that streams into an already recorded immutable-stage inode. It validates source/root/parent identities, stage/private-directory/managed-root identities, and the stage's single-link ownership before mutation. Source bytes are never consumed. Cancellation or failure preserves the owned inode and any partial bytes for later recovery. Final success checks the actual staged digest and size through its retained descriptor; shared publication remains a separate exclusive operation. These native-only methods are not exposed through the raw JavaScript file-operation allowlist.

Twenty-three focused stage tests and 54 existing file/installer/job/actual-JavaScriptCore cases passed on the Mac (77 total, zero skips). They cover empty and above-16-MiB streaming, cold retry, same-hash inode replacement, directory replacement, symlinks, hard links, collisions, cancellation and tampering during copy or final verification. Review found and corrected an initial result-proof gap: a callback could change an earlier copied chunk before the final stage hash was added. The corrected signed iPhone development build passed; no installation or physical interaction is claimed. Shared binding tests, affected core tests, typecheck, bundles and boot gates also passed after merging current remote changes. Durable session integration, prepared file Save, cleanup settlement and picker/viewer UI remain open.

### iOS exact editor checkpoint prerequisite (2026-10-05)

Internal exact checkpoint replacement and discard now compare the complete retained snapshot, including opaque UTF-8 payload bytes, and refuse pending Save attempts. Replays accept only the exact resulting checkpoint; later edits and corrupt evidence remain untouched. Mac validation: 18 focused tests and 25 existing JavaScriptCore editor recovery tests passed. These helpers have no attachment lifecycle or UI caller yet; durable file draft ownership, Save/Discard settlement, and picker/viewer integration remain open.

### iOS attachment publication recovery proof (2026-10-05)

A native-only helper now rechecks an interrupted publication using the recorded stage inode, streamed content hash and size, and managed-directory identity. Equal-hash files with different identities are refused; uncertain stages/namespaces are retained. Mac validation: 12 publication-proof cases plus 48 existing stage/file tests passed. The durable draft coordinator and editor integration remain pending.

### iOS attachment draft file jobs (2026-10-05)

Native-only source proofs, stage reservation/fill, exclusive publication, and publication verification now share the existing bounded FIFO, cancellation, reply retention, and shutdown drain. Raw JavaScript file-operation allowlists are unchanged. Mac validation: 15 new queue tests, 17 existing file-job/installer tests, and 12 JavaScriptCore host tests passed. Durable editor coordination remains unbound.

### iOS private attachment draft evidence store (2026-10-05)

A bounded private record now retains versioned operation phases, exact editor checkpoints, and file proofs. Writes refuse corrupt evidence, phase regression, replacement of immutable proofs, or removal of prior operations; reads reject symlinks and nonregular leaves without blocking on FIFOs. Mac validation: 25 record tests and 18 exact-checkpoint tests passed. This structural store remains unbound to editor lifecycle and grants no filesystem ownership by itself.

### iOS shared attachment draft projection (2026-10-05)

Shared helpers now validate an initial editor checkpoint, prepare an Add using RN's existing metadata and naming rules, and validate its frozen result without generating new IDs or timestamps. Ordered additions preserve prior attachments and all other editor fields. Current upload policy is checked at completion; historical additions remain structurally validated without applying a newer policy retroactively. Native file ownership remains a separate responsibility.

Validation: 84 new projection cases and 87 existing editor/file cases passed, along with core typecheck and scoped lint. Native lifecycle integration, file-aware Save, cleanup settlement, picker and viewer remain open; this shared slice alone makes no device or filesystem recovery claim.

### iOS durable attachment draft host (2026-10-05)

The internal native host now records Add intent, source and stage identities, publication proof, frozen shared metadata, and the exact editor checkpoint before acknowledging success. Cold recovery resumes only proven phases; equal-hash replacement files and unknown reservation identities are retained and refused. Discard can detach the exact checkpoint while retaining all pending file evidence, including interrupted Adds. It performs no file cleanup. Legacy editor writes and new journaled mutations are conservatively gated while this internal evidence exists; ordinary reads and compatible pre-existing journal recovery remain available.

A pure shared lineage validator also supports reopening an already extended draft without generating metadata or reapplying current upload policy to historical additions. Shared validation passed 198 cases. On the Mac, 28 new actual-JavaScriptCore draft-host cases, 21 typed file-job cases, and 37 existing editor/attachment regressions passed (86 total, zero skips). These include cold phase recovery, cancellation/drain, changed sources and targets, exact-request replay, corrupt evidence, retained pending Discard, capacity reservation, and conflicting editor-journal cleanup. Review corrected pending-Discard admission and rejected editor-terminal cleanup ordering. Both native bundles, boot gates, core typecheck and scoped TypeScript lint passed.

The signed iPhone 12 development build passed; it was not installed for this internal slice. File-aware task Save, Remove, durable cleanup and ownership release, picker/provider access, viewer UI, remote attachment sync and legacy byte migration remain open. An editor checkpoint after a durable result but before its completion marker must reconcile before Discard. There is no picker/editor UI binding yet, and these checks do not establish physical interaction or production upgrade acceptance.

### iOS recorded publication retirement primitive (2026-10-05)

A native-only helper can now remove one published file after checking its recorded inode, managed-directory identity, content and size through retained descriptors. A missing leaf counts as durable absence only after checking the original directory and syncing it; missing or replaced directories refuse. Cancellation before unlink preserves the file, while cancellation afterward cannot hide a completed removal. Failed durability remains uncertain and can be retried without adopting a replacement file.

Thirty focused cases and 60 existing file cases passed on the Mac, and the signed iPhone development build passed. No installation is claimed. This helper is not bound to JavaScript or the editor lifecycle: the eventual caller still needs a durable cleanup decision and a current shared reference check. Save/Discard cleanup and ownership release remain open.

### iOS owned-file Save authority (2026-10-05)

A separate shared prepare/validate/commit path now accepts frozen file additions and an exact attachment-only checkpoint. It reuses RN attachment merging and the existing full-task comparison and durable-save machinery. Existing URL-only Save contracts remain unchanged. An uncertain commit can replay only against its exact before/after task state; an intervening edit is retained and refused rather than overwritten.

Validation: 269 distinct shared cases passed, with core typecheck, scoped lint, both native bundles and boot gates. The rebuilt bundle passed 65 native host attachment/editor-recovery tests on the Mac, with no skips. Independent review passed. This is shared infrastructure: full editor checkpoint correspondence, native Save lifecycle, file Remove and UI remain unfinished.

### iOS owned private-stage retirement (2026-10-05)

The shared Apple installer now offers a separate native-only retirement entry for recorded private stages. It checks the recorded managed root, private directory and stage identities, refuses replacements and hardlinks, and confirms parent-directory durability before acknowledging removal or an already-missing stage. It never recreates a missing root. The existing RN cleanup entry retains its behavior.

Validation: 71 Apple package tests passed (32 new, 39 existing), no skips; the signed iPhone development build passed. Independent review passed. No phone installation or user-facing cleanup acceptance is claimed. Native lifecycle ownership and the latest shared live-reference check are still required before this primitive may delete draft files.

### iOS typed retirement transport (2026-10-05)

Recorded publication and private-stage retirement now run through the native attachment FIFO with bounded proof inputs and isolated typed replies. Cancellation before work preserves bytes; after deletion begins the operation finishes and reports its actual durability outcome. Raw JavaScript file APIs remain unchanged.

Validation: 93 native Mac tests passed with no skips, including 25 new queue cases; signed iPhone development build and independent review passed. These internal jobs remain unbound: the coordinator must still retain a durable cleanup decision and check current shared live references before using them. No new phone UI behavior is claimed.

### iOS attachment history between editor checkpoints (2026-10-05)

A separate shared version2 history path permits ordinary editor changes between frozen file additions while requiring unchanged attachment baselines and ordered attachment lists. Each addition retains its original complete metadata and before/after payloads. Version1 keeps its exact checkpoint chain; neither API admits file removal through this change. Native filesystem ownership and full Save admission remain separate checks.

Validation: 142 shared cases passed (111 existing, 31 new), with typecheck, scoped lint, both bundles and boot gates. The rebuilt bundle passed 65 native attachment/editor-recovery regression tests with no skips. Independent review passed. Native version2 checkpoint routing and the file UI remain unfinished.

### iOS ordinary editor Save correspondence (2026-10-05)

A shared validator now checks the full ordinary editor checkpoint against its explicit Save request and frozen opening task, including touched no-op fields, raw text/token buffers, schedule queues, field groups and attachment lists. It reuses existing opening-field and recurrence-display helpers. Pending, unknown, checklist and lifecycle inputs are refused intact; this ordinary route does not cover their separate Save semantics.

The final focused suite passed122 cases; the earlier eight-suite run passed323 cases before the token-only review correction. Core typecheck, scoped lint and independent review passed. The helper is unbound and does not itself grant Save or filesystem authority.

### iOS durable owned editor checkpoint storage (2026-10-05)

Private version2 records now retain exact before/after checkpoint intent while ordinary editor fields advance between file additions. The editor file accepts only the recorded before or after; retrying the exact after rewrites it durably before acknowledgment. Complete encoded-record capacity is checked before mutation. Existing version1 records retain their original contract.

Validation: 83 focused Mac tests passed with no skips, independent review passed, and the signed iPhone development build passed. The permission-failure tests cover pre-write refusal and cold retry, not injected failure after rename. No phone installation is claimed. Native routing is being integrated separately; this storage change does not enable file-aware Save or the attachment UI.

### iOS combined ordinary editor and owned-file Save authority (2026-10-05)

A separate shared Save entry now combines ordinary editor fields with privately recorded file additions. Every prepared Save checks the complete raw checkpoint against the explicit request and frozen opening task, then uses the existing RN merge and full-task durable comparison. Old URL-only and attachment-only entries retain their original scope. Checklist/lifecycle edits and file removal remain separate work.

Validation: 464 shared tests passed (71 new and 393 existing), including actual SQLite lost-acknowledgment recovery, cold replay, intervening-edit refusal, metadata preservation and input-hook refusal. Typecheck, scoped lint, both bundles, boot gates and independent review passed. Native Save admission, exact editor cleanup, file ownership release and user-facing attachment controls remain unfinished; these checks do not establish native or physical-device Save acceptance.

### iOS owned editor checkpoint recovery (2026-10-05)

The native host now offers a distinct version2 Begin and routes ordinary checkpoints through retained before/after intent, exact editor-file comparison and durable completion. Frozen Add history survives intervening ordinary edits; retrying an older Add returns its retained reply without rolling back the latest checkpoint. Cold recovery accepts only the recorded before/after state. Existing version1 behavior and mutation gates remain intact.

Validation: 25 new JavaScriptCore cases passed across the full run and one focused fixture correction, plus 65 existing attachment/editor recovery cases. The correction recreated the host after external SQL fixture changes so shared read-only state was actually loaded; no product policy changed.
