Save, ownership release and picker/editor UI remain separate work.

### iOS exact owned Save storage primitives (2026-10-05)

Separate native primitives now freeze, thaw and detach an owned Save only when the complete checkpoint and attempt match byte for byte. A canonical hash binds every field of the retained file evidence; release removes only that exact record and confirms directory durability on absent retries. Existing record schemas and legacy editor operations remain unchanged.

Validation: 113 store tests passed with zero skips (30 new, 83 existing), independent review passed, and the signed development build passed without installation. These are structural primitives: the caller must still prove the Save result or nonapplication and complete file cleanup before releasing ownership. Native Save integration is separate work.

### iOS ordinary editor Save with owned file additions (2026-10-05)

The native host now combines the complete ordinary editor request with its retained file additions through the shared RN-derived Save policy. It freezes one exact request, persists the pending command before applying it, and records a validated durable result before detaching the editor, retiring private staging directories and releasing ownership. Published task files and borrowed picker sources remain untouched. A confirmed Save with unfinished cleanup has a distinct retry result; retry performs cleanup without rewriting the task.

Validation: 23 new real JavaScriptCore/SQLite/file tests passed together against the merged shared bundle, including cold recovery across Save boundaries, actual SQLite acknowledgment loss followed by native reply loss, intervening task edits, exact authority checks and escaped-capacity refusal before freezing. Another 78 existing attachment/editor recovery tests passed on the preceding implementation snapshot; the final change adds an extra native-authority check after journal removal and is covered by the new suite. Typecheck, scoped lint, bundle startup and diagnostic-field checks passed. The signed development build passed without installation. Independent review passed. This internal API does not establish picker/editor UI, file Remove, terminal Discard cleanup or physical-device Save acceptance.

### iOS owned checkpoint reservation for Discard (2026-10-05)

New ordinary checkpoint advances now measure the exact future Discard decision and detached record before accepting the change. This prevents a growing raw editor payload from consuming the space needed to record Discard. Recovery of an older pending checkpoint retains its original admission rules and completes the exact recorded transition.

Validation: two new real JavaScriptCore/file cases passed using actual encoded histories at the capacity boundary, and all 25 existing checkpoint recovery tests passed. The tests preserve editor bytes, file proofs and unrelated database rows on refusal; a fitting checkpoint can still Discard after restart. Older pending recovery emits no new capacity-reservation marker. Bundle startup, scoped lint, diagnostic-field checks and the signed development build passed without installation. Independent review passed. Terminal file cleanup and the attachment picker remain separate work.

### iOS shared owned-Add Discard candidates (2026-10-05)

A pure shared helper now validates retained version1/version2 Add history and derives Discard candidates through the existing RN settlement planner. It preserves opening baseline files and tombstones, keeps pending Add proposals distinct from acknowledged checkpoints, and returns only the exact frozen new-Add identities and target URIs. Candidates do not prove that a copy exists or authorize deletion.

Validation: 206 shared tests passed (64 new, 142 existing), with typecheck and scoped lint passing. Both native bundles rebuilt successfully; the iOS bundle remained byte-identical because this helper is not bound to a native caller yet. Independent review passed. Native live-reference checks, file retirement, terminal ownership release and picker UI remain separate work.

### iOS exact detached Discard record release (2026-10-05)

Separate storage primitives now bind a complete detached Discard record to its exact canonical hash and release only that matching record. Request and reply identities must match the retained checkpoint. Active or merely decided records, changed opaque fields and nonregular paths refuse release; an already absent record still requires directory durability. Existing Save-only release remains separate.

Validation: 19 new file-backed tests and 95 existing store tests passed with zero skips, independent review passed, and the signed development build passed without installation. These structural primitives do not prove file retirement or current live-reference checks. ### iOS Discard candidate and current-reference bindings (2026-10-05)

The iOS host now binds the shared RN-derived Discard candidate planner and a synchronous current-reference check. The check requires loaded, settled personal storage with no active writes or edit locks, examines all task and project references, and invokes one native-held callback before JavaScriptCore returns to Swift. It does not itself grant file ownership or authorize arbitrary paths.

Validation: 67 new binding cases and existing boot gates passed, both native bundles built, scoped binding lint passed, and independent review passed. The existing boot script has no new lint violations against its baseline. These binding tests use controlled persistence state; native file proof, callback lifetime and cold recovery acceptance remain separate work. No attachment UI or physical-device acceptance is claimed.

### iOS terminal Discard for published owned additions (2026-10-05)

The native host now finishes an exact detached Discard when every addition has retained publication proof, or when there are no additions. It journals the operation, checks current settled task/project references through the shared RN policy, and retires only proof-bound unreferenced targets and their private stages. Native retirement completes inside the synchronous JavaScript callback before return-time microtasks. A durable completion result precedes sidecar release and final journal removal; replay of that result starts no file jobs. Borrowed sources and opening baseline files remain untouched.

Validation: all 24 new real JavaScriptCore/SQLite/file cases passed together, covering cold interruption boundaries, lost unlink acknowledgment, archived/read-only references, a newly persisted reference, callback misuse/order, evidence replacement, cancellation/drain and malformed/nonregular journals. All 48 existing Save/editor recovery cases passed on the preceding native snapshot; the final four-line change strengthens the Discard-only journal replacement check and is covered by the new suite. Binding, diagnostics, signed development build and independent review passed; no phone installation is claimed. Incomplete publication phases remain retained and refuse before a new cleanup journal. Picker UI, file removal and the ordinary-work policy for retained cleanup remain separate work.

### iOS Discard of a recorded unfinished copy (2026-10-05)

Discard can now retire the final recorded private stage when copying stopped before its completion marker, including empty, partial and fully copied stages. Earlier published additions keep the existing live-reference and ownership checks. The new result distinguishes an untouched public target from a removed or missing stage; existing path confinement and symlink checks remain. Intent without recorded stage ownership and ambiguous publication still retain their evidence.

Validation: all 29 real JavaScriptCore/file Discard cases passed together with zero skips. Five new cases cover both record versions, actual streaming cancellation, lost copy acknowledgment, ordinary edits and published prefixes, missing source, foreign target preservation, cold replay, proof/namespace conflicts and sealed result versions. A fixture was corrected to reflect that the existing remover can unlink its proven-owned stage before refusing to remove a directory containing an unrelated child; the child and unresolved evidence are preserved. Signed development build and independent review passed without phone installation. Picker UI remains separate work.

### iOS logical Discard of an unstarted file addition (2026-10-05)

An exact detached Discard can now release a final Add whose durable phase is still intent, after draining native file work and rechecking the complete record. Earlier checkpointed additions retain the existing live-reference and ownership checks. The new result explicitly says `discarded`, with the last target `untouched` and stage `unclaimed`; it performs no file operations on that last namespace. Unknown leftover files may remain, and this result makes no claim that they were removed or empty. Copied or ambiguously published additions cannot use this path.

Validation: all 36 real JavaScriptCore/file Discard cases passed together with zero skips, including seven new cases across both history formats, normal reservation crash windows, cancellation and drain, unknown nonempty namespaces, external record rollback, restored prefix references, cold journal/release boundaries, strict result versions and existing mutation gates. Binding startup, diagnostics ledger, signed development build and independent review passed. No phone installation or picker UI acceptance is claimed.

### iOS exact filled-stage observation (2026-10-05)

A native-only typed file operation can now positively observe an existing filled private stage against its recorded managed directory, private directory and file inode, content hash and size. It rechecks descriptor and path stability around cancellation and hashing, without opening the borrowed source or public target, creating directories, or changing files. Missing or conflicting evidence remains unclassified. This observation does not itself authorize cleanup.

Validation: 57 native file tests passed with zero skips, including 17 new observer cases and existing fill, publication and installer regressions; all 71 shared installer tests passed. Review also found that Swift's regex end anchor accepted trailing newlines: all five matching native/shared-installer path guards now require the absolute end, with real-inode LF/CRLF refusal coverage. Signed development build and independent review passed without phone installation. Discard integration and picker UI remain separate work.

### iOS Discard after a filled copy or lost publication acknowledgment (2026-10-05)

New Discard admission first tries to prove that the exact recorded filled stage is still present. A positive observation records a version4 private-retirement decision without reading or changing the public target. If that observation fails, only a separate positive publication proof can select target cleanup: the native owner durably retains the proof and binds a fresh journal to the updated record. If neither proof succeeds, it retains the evidence. An already recorded private-retirement decision retries safely after lost unlink acknowledgment without reclassifying a missing stage as publication failure.

Validation: all 44 real JavaScriptCore/file Discard tests passed together with zero skips. Eight new cases and one adapted case cover both record histories, missing borrowed sources, foreign targets, live references, exact proof promotion, cold cleanup replay, strict result versions and cancellation before journaling. Review found and corrected the complete authority check after the final pre-journal hook for all fresh versions. Signed development build and independent review passed without phone installation. These are internal recovery APIs; file Remove and picker/editor UI remain separate work.

A native-only observer now captures the measured content and inode of an eligible existing managed file. Missing files grant no right to delete a later generation; observation creates no directories and performs no deletion. Managed filenames must match the flat ID-name rule.

### Complete owned editor Save contract (2026-10-05)

The shared owned Save contract now reuses the existing checklist and lifecycle planner for full editor changes, including completion, recurring tasks, Cancel, Skip, and cancellation Undo. It preserves complete affected-row persistence and replay checks, concurrent attachment metadata, and the original cancellation identity. A valid empty file-operation history can save ordinary edits without gaining file-cleanup ownership; an unchanged save does not stamp a new revision or initialize a device ID. Older request grammars remain separate.

Validation: 20 focused real SQLite contract tests passed, covering complete edits, generated recurring tasks, failed commits and lost acknowledgments, cold replay, later edits, raw checkpoint correspondence, empty histories, malformed Undo requests, and unchanged saves. The 590 existing affected cases passed across the full run and one targeted rerun after giving a real persistence-backoff test a scoped 30-second limit; typecheck and scoped lint passed. Five existing native checklist/cancellation recovery tests also passed. This is a shared prerequisite: the new contract is not yet bound to the native journal, editor UI, or physical picker.


### Native provider-file ingestion (2026-10-05)

The internal iOS provider facade now coordinates a selected file, streams at most 50 MiB into a private copied source, and uses the existing V3 Add owner. It retains original filename/MIME metadata and exact file identities, checks editor ownership after copying, and preserves copied bytes while interrupted Add recovery may still need them. Only native creation evidence permits scratch cleanup; borrowed files and provider originals remain untouched. The optional completion diagnostic runs after cleanup so it cannot introduce an intervening ownership change.

Validation: all 20 new native/JSC tests passed, including measured size boundaries, metadata, cancellation, warm/cold recovery, foreign-file preservation, cleanup failures, and diagnostic ordering. The 22 existing mixed Add tests and five checklist/cancellation recovery cases passed. Bridge startup checks, diagnostic checks, both bundles, scoped lint, independent review and the signed development build passed. Test setup, raw SQL object ordering, cancellation error expectations and asynchronous arrival waits were corrected without weakening assertions or file authority.

A process crash can leave an unproven copy in the library-backed cache. No filename or age sweep deletes it, and total accumulation is not bounded; this cache has no guaranteed OS reclamation. This implementation is an internal host prerequisite. External document-provider behavior, the picker UI, and physical-device acceptance are not established by local coordinated-file tests.

Remote files, hashless relocation, pending-history recovery, full signed RN-to-native upgrades and broader accessibility/iPad acceptance remain separate gates.

### iOS processed photo Save recovery (2026-10-06, Task286)

One actual UIKit producer/host regression now joins Photo Add to interrupted complete Save and cold recovery. An injected failure after the durable `domainSaved` terminal retains the exact Save request, original Add identity, editor and ownership records. After the borrowed provider file is removed, a recreated host returns the original saved receipt, retires cleanup and opens the same processed image. Guards reject a new preparation or another Task write during recovery; the saved revision, unrelated Task, processed bytes and target identity stay unchanged. A second cold start has no recovery receipt or pending sidecars.

All 25 UIKit photo codec/provider checks passed with no failures or skips in 7.485 seconds; the new recovery case passed in 1.327 seconds. Initial runs caught a nonexistent fixture column and an incorrect assumption that normal Photo Add already stores `fileHash`; these test assumptions were corrected without changing production behavior or weakening the processed-byte comparison. The current hashless metadata limitation remains relevant to Task285 relocation. This is simulator UIKit/JSC/SQLite recovery evidence, not new physical-device failure-UI acceptance. Existing Task283 normal photo Save/device evidence remains separate.

### iOS verified hashes on new Project file imports (2026-10-06, Task287)

New native Project file Add now freezes the existing verified source SHA-256 into attachment metadata before the coordinated Project write. The explicit new request/preparation/native-state versions bind that digest to the source, completed metadata and existing publication proof; interrupted cold recovery uses the same frozen operation. Historical hashless journals retain their original grammar and recovery behavior, with no backfill or malformed-new fallback. This enables Task285's strict settled-file relocation opener for newly imported Project files. RN initial Add is also hashless; this is a native addition, not a claim of fixing an RN regression. Task and photo histories remain separate work.

The historical fixture does not claim execution of an older application binary. Governance passed 280 tests and 2,179 assertions.

Fresh native Task file drafts now retain the existing verified source SHA-256 in completed attachment metadata. Photos hash the processed image bytes. RN picker, callback ownership and Save cleanup behavior informed the implementation; RN initial Add is also hashless.

### iOS HTTP transport foundation (2026-10-06, Task290)

The native host now supplies the existing JavaScript fetch bridge through an ephemeral URLSession and the shared file/network reply queue. Responses complete in full or fail; cancellation and host shutdown drain registered callbacks before releasing the library lock. Opaque network IDs and a single body-source selection keep file and HTTP replies separate. Shared RN/core transport behavior and Android's host implementation informed the port.

This initial transport accepts HTTPS, up to 8 MiB per upload/download and two retained jobs, with a 300-second resource timeout. It requests identity encoding and refuses nonempty compressed responses. Followed redirects are limited to five same-origin hops; manual/error modes and unsafe destinations do not follow. Explicit Authorization is restored only after the existing same-origin guards pass. There are no persisted cookies, credential stores, cache, retries or TLS exceptions. These limits are intentionally narrower than general sync/attachment support. KV, Sync, AI and remote attachment binding remain unavailable; this adds no network settings or automatic network feature.

The final 12 JavaScriptCore/URLSession tests passed without skips in 3.161 seconds. They include controlled real TCP truncation, reset, stalled-body timeout, gzip refusal, manual/error/cross-origin redirects, the five-hop limit, 307 PUT bytes/auth/conditional-header preservation and unchanged self-signed TLS rejection. Other cases cover strict admission, binary/text data, response bounds, cancellation, callback drain and library locking, file/network reply separation, optional-file failure, startup silence and all-table preservation. The existing file-host suite also passed all 12 cases. Review corrected an insufficient shutdown barrier; the expanded real-wire test then reproduced URLSession dropping explicit Authorization on a same-origin 307, which the final code corrects. Boot, typecheck, diagnostic-field checks and 282 governance tests passed; scoped lint passed with two unchanged baseline regex violations excluded.

The final signed iPhone12/iOS17.5.1 build passed startup and existing document Share/photo QuickLook opening. All 21 fixture tables and three managed file hashes remained identical, with no pending sidecars. All 21 ordinary development tables and parsed preferences matched their baseline, and the ordinary Inbox was restored. The fixed `v1.3.5/ios-http-transport` diagnostic proves current-runtime complete body delivery without logging URLs, headers, identifiers or contents. Physical HTTP/sync, large transfers, compressed responses, Keychain/crypto/KV integration and full signed RN-to-native upgrade acceptance remain separate work.


### iOS secure-storage transport foundation (2026-10-06, Task291)

The native host now implements the existing asynchronous secret bridge with Security.framework on a serial worker. It preserves RN/Expo's byte-encoded account/generic attributes, ordered no-auth/auth/legacy lookup, and value-only updates that retain an existing item's accessibility. New writes receive the shared core's requested accessibility; existing two-argument callers default to when-unlocked. Android retains its previous request shape. Only confirmed item-not-found becomes null; other Keychain errors reject without prompting or exposing credential contents. Explicit deletion checks all three aliases for the selected account. This first slice retains older aliases after a new write instead of performing Expo's write-induced cleanup.

Two retained replies and strict input/value bounds constrain the bridge. Accepted synchronous Keychain work finishes before library ownership is released. Cancellation still permits the shared sync facade's awaited cleanup while refusing ordinary secret calls; host close refuses new work. Startup performs no Keychain operation. KV, Sync and AI remain unavailable. The fixed `v1.3.5/ios-secure-storage` diagnostic records successful native result delivery without credential names or values.

All 12 focused tests passed on the signed development app on iPhone12/iOS17.5.1, using unique synthetic Keychain namespaces. They cover real writes/reads, empty and Unicode values, host recreation, accessibility and update preservation, Expo-shaped legacy records, unrelated-account preservation, strict refusal, malformed stored data, mixed file/HTTP/secret delivery, four sequential awaited cleanup writes after cancellation, and close-time library exclusion. Injected locked/unavailable/authentication statuses test error classification; they do not establish behavior on a physically locked device. Earlier macOS default-Keychain tests and unsigned simulator runners failed and are not acceptance evidence; the suite requires an entitled iOS app host and explicitly skips on macOS. Two fixture path corrections were needed for iOS compilation and its writable Application Support directory; feature assertions were retained.

The 22 existing file/HTTP host cases passed separately. Boot, core typecheck, diagnostic-field checks and scoped lint passed. All 21 ordinary development tables and parsed preferences remained unchanged after the physical tests, with no pending sidecars; the ordinary development app was relaunched. Production RN-to-native access-group compatibility, real locked-device behavior, crypto, KV integration and enabled sync remain separate gates.

### iOS encryption primitive foundation (2026-10-06, Task292)

The standalone host now implements the existing asynchronous Argon2id and AES-256-GCM bridge. Argon2id uses RN QuickCrypto 1.1.7's checksum-pinned OpenSSL 3.6.2 binary through a small C adapter; CryptoKit supplies AES-GCM. Shared core still owns NFC normalization and the MWENC1 format, including authenticated headers and tolerated trailing padding. Strict input/cost limits, two retained jobs, generation-bound replies and close-time draining keep primitive work off the Engine queue. Failed body delivery now rejects its existing promise instead of leaving it unresolved. A fixed Diagnostics receipt records successful delivery without cryptographic input or output.

All 17 focused tests passed on macOS/JSC and the signed iPhone 12/iOS 17.5.1 development app. They cover the shared eight Argon2 and seven AES vectors, five encrypted-container fixtures, authentication failures, input limits, mixed file/HTTP/secret/crypto replies, cancellation and shutdown, and unchanged database contents. A runtime crash exposed cleanup using zero-based offsets on CryptoKit Data slices; the shared cleanup now uses each buffer's actual indices. The original preservation assertions pass with that correction, and its direct-worker regression passed Address Sanitizer. Full-host sanitizer execution remains blocked before crypto by an existing large validator's instrumented stack usage. Separately, 22 existing file/HTTP cases passed; their two real-network cases were not repeated.

CI builds a separate crypto test bundle; test exports and fixtures are excluded from the ordinary application bundle. This foundation does not enable Sync or AI. Device KV, credential migration, network/lifecycle integration, sync attachment ownership, provider support and signed RN replacement still need acceptance. The primitive plaintext limit is 8 MiB; later Sync admission must account for MWENC1 overhead against the transport's 8 MiB body limit.

### iOS device settings storage foundation (2026-10-06, Task293)

An internal Swift store now implements bounded RN AsyncStorage-compatible reads and atomic nonsecret settings batches in an explicitly supplied current iOS namespace. It reuses the strict legacy reader, preserves exact UTF-8 keys and values, keeps unknown records and external value files, and does not change the legacy import writer. Native-written values are inline; RN accepts inline values beyond its usual write threshold. A fixed namespace lease and retained directory/file descriptors prevent a second native owner or replaced paths from gaining write authority. A failed acknowledgment retains the exact operation; a retry accepts only the captured before-state or its own published inode and intended contents.

Mutations are limited to the selected nonsecret sync-configuration/status keys, 64 entries per call, 4 KiB keys, 1 MiB values and 8 MiB batch input. Source manifest/snapshot limits remain 64/256 MiB. Existing external files are retained, so this does not implement plaintext-secret retirement. Populated historical Documents copies require separate migration. This class is not yet bound to CoreHost or an app screen. The shared host explicitly prevents iOS KV presence from activating the Android Sync/AI factory or replacing accepted local attachment handling. UserDefaults, credentials and domain data retain their current owners.

Validation: final Mac native 52/52 tests passed, three synthetic JavaScriptCore legacy-upgrade tests passed, and the signed iPhone 12 on iOS 17.5.1 passed all 27 storage tests without skips. Device tests compare external UTF-8 text with the exact RN NSString file reader and verify that an existing stronger manifest protection class survives replacement. This is not locked-device or production-RN-upgrade acceptance. Independent bounded review: SHIP. The ordinary development app was restored without resetting its data; all 21 database tables and preferences matched the pre-test snapshot, with no journal sidecars left behind.

### iOS explicit device-storage host bridge (2026-10-06, Task294)

CoreHost can now receive an explicit device-storage container and bundle identifier. Without that argument, its six KV methods remain absent; the ordinary App and legacy rehearsal continue using that default. An unavailable optional namespace refuses storage requests without blocking a valid library startup or substituting empty data. All operations run on the existing host queue and obey its recovery, pending-write and attachment ownership guards. Closing the host finishes accepted synchronous work before releasing the namespace and library leases.

The bridge validates primitive strings and exact batch shapes, preserves ordered duplicates and missing versus empty values, and returns fixed errors without names, contents or paths. Input frames are limited to 12 MiB. Replies allow 1 MiB per value, 8 MiB of decoded UTF-8 strings including keys, and 12 MiB serialized; oversized replies are refused as a whole. The underlying store's mutation bounds and allowlist remain unchanged. A current-runtime successful delivery emits the fixed forced Diagnostics receipt `v1.3.5/ios-device-storage`; startup, refusal and stale delivery do not. Sync, AI, remote attachments and ordinary App namespace activation remain pending.

Validation: all 12 actual JavaScriptCore host tests passed on the Mac and all 12 passed on the signed iPhone 12 running iOS 17.5.1, without skips. They cover all six wire methods, unknown/external data preservation, Unicode/BOM handling, malformed and oversized input/output, namespace contention, corrupt optional storage, exact lost-acknowledgment retry, cold reopening, preboot refusal, fixed Diagnostics delivery, close ownership, and refusal during an ordinary task completion or real attachment draft. Independent bounded review: SHIP. These checks do not close the full Sync or signed RN-upgrade gates.

### iOS legacy sync-credential entry removal (2026-10-06, Task296)

The explicit device-storage port now permits removal, but never plaintext writes, for the three existing legacy sync-credential keys. A request can contain only one selected credential; mixed and duplicate batches are rejected before writes. Removal first durably publishes the manifest without that entry, then verifies and unlinks only its captured RN MD5 backing path and synchronizes the directory. Unknown entries, unrelated files and replaced paths remain protected by the existing namespace and descriptor checks. Orphan-file capture includes the legacy reader's full input and prospective manifest encoding in the byte budget. Ordinary nonsecret mutations keep their previous read cost.

An interrupted operation retains its exact warm retry and refuses unrelated requests. Cold opening performs no cleanup: after a manifest-only interruption, reads see the absent entry while the backing file remains; a new explicit removal completes cleanup. Already-absent entry and file are a successful no-op. RN's current backing-file removal ignores filesystem errors; the native port deliberately requires durable acknowledgment instead. This is removal from the canonical RN namespace, not forensic erasure, deletion from backups, or proof of Keychain migration. The ordinary App, Sync and AI remain unbound.

The fixed `v1.3.5/ios-legacy-secret-retirement` Diagnostics receipt proves successful delivery of a selected removal, including a no-op, without credential names, values, paths or identifiers. Startup, refusal and stale runtime delivery produce no success.

Validation: 18 focused tests passed on the Mac and the signed iPhone 12 running iOS 17.5.1, without skips. They include 14 storage fault/preservation cases and four actual JavaScriptCore cases. The latter verify exact persisted Diagnostics contexts, privacy, warm retry, and recreation before explicit orphan cleanup. The existing 38 storage/host regression tests passed separately. Full shared boot gates, four Diagnostics field tests, core typecheck and scoped lint passed; the pre-existing control-regex lint rule was excluded. The first expanded run retained four setup failures caused by a test-only credential namespace prefix; the corrected final tests passed with the production Security guard unchanged. The ordinary development app was restored without a reset; all 21 database tables and preferences matched the pre-Task293 snapshot, with no journal sidecars. Independent bounded review: SHIP.

### iOS bounded WebDAV upload admission prerequisite (2026-10-06, Tasks299–300)

The existing shared WebDAV factory accepts an optional plaintext upload-byte limit. Actual source and immutable-snapshot size govern admission before migration, copy, hashing or upload; advisory attachment metadata cannot bypass it. An unmeasurable selected source also refuses. An unchanged cloud attachment whose recorded content stat matches needs no read or upload and can remain above the limit. Callers without the capability retain their existing behavior, including File Sync. A future encrypted caller must reserve the encryption envelope inside its transport limit. The native local Add limit remains 50 MiB.

A refused upload aborts the pass with a distinct fatal error. Both SyncRun attachment phases return failure even under warning policy; refusal cannot become an unreadable-file tombstone or File Sync's successful blocked result. The pass returns no accumulated patches, but does not roll back earlier confirmed remote uploads. The fixed `v1.3.5/webdav-host-upload-limit` diagnostic is best-effort: a logging exception cannot replace the fatal error. The reviewed logging regression reproduced false success in both phases before the correction.

Both focused tests passed on macOS JavaScriptCore and the signed iPhone 12 running iOS 17.5.1, with zero skips. They verify a real 16 MiB + 1 byte source refused before native byte reads, prepare/post-merge refusal across host recreation, unchanged database rows/source inode/hash/cache, fixed persisted Diagnostics, and a nine-byte exact-cap upload through real native snapshots and intercepted URLSession with the expected binary body and create-only condition. ### iOS Task download recovery record (2026-10-07, Task357)

The selected native availability record now distinguishes terminal metadata-only resolution, a borrowed existing file, and a newly owned publication. It binds the frozen shared proof to the exact editor snapshots and retained native receipts, forbids promotion from borrowed to owned, and preserves acknowledged receipt fields through retries. Acknowledgments include the sidecar bytes and filesystem identity; replacing a sidecar with identical bytes does not preserve its owner. Corrupt, opposite-version and unsafe sidecars remain untouched.

Validation: 152 Mac tests passed, including 134 historical Store controls and 18 new cases. The 18 new cases also passed on the physical iPhone 12. Tests cover strict decoding, receipt and phase continuity, checkpoint/Discard progression, escaped-byte bounds, and inode/hardlink replacement. Independent review found no blocking issues. The receipts in these Store tests are structural fixtures, not proof of installer ownership.

Ordinary boot still rejects this new record version, and Task Download remains disabled. Shared-policy recomputation, native Save/Resume/Discard consumers, physical publication recovery and download admission must be complete before activation.

### iOS encryption Enable output-capacity prerequisite (2026-10-07, Task376)

The iOS WebDAV encryption service now supplies its existing 8 MiB transport limit to the shared Enable writer. Before changing any artifact, encryption key or transition journal, the writer checks the complete captured inventory against the canonical MWENC1 output size, including its header and authentication tag. A late oversized attachment or document refuses before earlier artifacts are converted. Authenticated foreign generations use their decrypted payload length when rewrapped; unchanged encrypted generations and provider padding retain the existing policy. RN, Android, File Sync and Dropbox omit this WebDAV capability and retain their previous behavior. Remote fence acquisition and release can precede a capacity refusal. The fixed `v1.3.5/ios-encryption-enable-capacity` diagnostic identifies this refusal without artifact contents or identifiers.

Validation passed: 180 core tests, 233 RN controls, 33 native-adapter tests, diagnostics fields, typecheck, scoped lint and the complete embedded-host startup gates. These tests use actual native Argon2/AES with an in-memory versioned remote: they prove early refusal, exact output boundaries, authenticated padding and interrupted rewrap retry, not real WebDAV conversion, durable encryption-state recovery, or physical iPhone acceptance. No App or host action was enabled; native Enable and the wider S06 workflow remain open.
